## Document 125: 8月工业企业利润数据点评：利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月工业企业利润数据点评：利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行

[Table_ReportType] 
——8 月工业企业利润数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 09 月 29 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 企业利润增速持续放缓，营收增速上行。8 月规上工业企业利
润累计同比继续下跌 1.9 个百分点至 15.7%，当月同比增速为
4.2%，前值为11.2%。营业收入增速由 6.5%上行至6.6%。从不
同类型企业来看，国企、股份制企业利润下滑提速，私企利润
持续放缓，外企利润边际修复，企业杠杆率整体小幅抬升。 
⚫ 营收利润率同比涨幅缩窄，工业品价格再度上行，工业生产增
速回温。8 月，PPI 同比由3.5%回升至 3.8%，输入性通胀压力
持续， 价格上下游传导依然受阻， 上下游企业利润或持续分化。
能源产业链价格预期仍将维持高位震荡，PPI 在油价支撑下预
期延续高位。 工业增加值同比由4.5%上行至5.2%， 工业生产边
际修复，其中高技术产业为主要拉动。营业收入利润率累计值
与上月的5.66%持平， 同比上涨0.42 个百分点，涨幅有所缩窄。 
⚫ 企业成本持续上行，费用压力 逐步缓解，应收账款回收期延长 。
每百元营业收入中的成本继续上涨 0.07 元至 85.07 元，上游资源
品价格走高给企业带来的影响持续 。企业每百元营业收入中的费
用由 8.41 元回落为 8.35 元， 减税降费等纾困政策效果逐步显现，
费用压力持续缓解。 应收账款同比上行， 产业链上下游企业博弈加
剧，带动应收账款账期拉长。 
⚫ 采矿业利润持续上行， 制造业结构分化，AI 驱动特征持续。煤炭
开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业增速上行，为采矿业主要
拉动。地产、基建投资尚未修复，上游黑色金属、非金属矿采
选业和加工制造业利润增速下滑。能化产业链分化持续，化纤
制造利润维持高位，化学原料增速持续回落。中游设备制造整
体利润回升。下游制造业中，计算机、通信等设备制造增速高
位上行，运输设备增速放缓。消费品制造业利润表现分化，食
品饮料加工增速放缓，医药、烟草、文娱用品等边际修复。 
⚫ “量价齐升”驱动营收上行，企业库存持续累积。营业收入在
工业生产回暖、工业品价格推动下回升。工业产成品库存持续
累积，但累库速度放缓。内需依然偏弱，资源品涨价给下游企
业带来成本压力，企业现金流和利润空间仍面临一定约束。未
来仍需关注PPI 以及上下游行业价格剪刀差变化，中下游制造
业企业成本刚性问题短期难以迎来实质性缓解。新经济利润表
现预期持续好于旧经济。海外政策未来仍存在一定波动，海外
货币政策短期收紧的趋势不变。我国政策仍将注重稳经济，四
季度财政资金有望逐步落地， “六张网”将发挥重要支撑作用。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预
期，消费、基建修复持续偏弱。

事件 
2026年1-8月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额5.27万亿元，累计同比较上月下
跌1.9个百分点至15.7%，8月当月同比增速由上月的11.2%回落为4.2%。

1    企业利润增速持续放缓，工业品价格再度上行 
企业利润增速持续回落， 营收增速高位上行， 营收利润率同比保持上涨。2026年8月，
全国规模以上工业企业利润累计同比由7月的17.6%继续回落至15.7%，当月同比增速
为4.2%， 前值为11.2%。规模以上企业营业收入累计同比由6.5%上行至6.6%，达到2022
年12月以来高点。营业收入利润率累计值与上月的5.66%持平，同比上涨0.42个百分
点，涨幅有所缩窄。 
工业品价格再度上行， 工业生产增速回温。8月，PPI同比由3.5%回升至3.8%， 累计同
比由1.8%上行至2%， 输入性通胀压力持续， 价格上下游传导依然受阻， 下游企业利润
相对承压，上下游企业利润或持续分化。能源产业链价格预期仍将维持高位震荡，PPI
在油价支撑下预期延续高位。 工业增加值同比由4.5%上行至5.2%， 工业生产边际修复，
其中高技术产业为主要拉动。

图表1：工业企业利润增速高位持续放缓（%） 图表2：拆分看，工业品价格上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

企业单位成本持续上行， 费用压力逐步缓解，但对冲有限， 应收账款回收期延长 。8月，
企业每百元资产实现的营业收入由72.2元上行至72.4元。每百元营业收入中的成本 继续
上涨0.07元至85.07元，企业成本刚性问题延续，上游资源品价格走高给企业带来的影响
持续。企业每百元营业收入中的费用由8.41元回落为8.35元，减税降费等纾困政策效果
逐步显现，费用压力持续缓解。 产成品周转天数持平于上期的21.3天。 规模以上工业企业
应收账款同比由7月的8.5%上行至9%，应收账款平均回收期环比由71.9天上行至72.2天，
产业链上下游企业博弈加剧，带动应收账款账期拉长。

国企、 股份制企业利润下滑提速，私企利润持续放缓， 外企利润边际修复，企业杠杆
率整体小幅抬升。2026年8月，分不同企业类型来看，国有企业利润累计同比增速由
上月的16.3%进一步下行至10.3%，下滑速度加快。股份制企业利润累计同比由23.6%
回落至20.4%，跌幅边际加大。私营企业由10.9%回落至10.4%，下滑速度放缓。外资
企业利润累计同比增速由1.2%回升至2.3%， 利润依然相对承压。 仅私企资产负债率与
上月持平，国企、外企和股份制企业资产负债率均延续小幅抬升。 
图表5：国企、股份制企业利润回落幅度较大（%） 图表6：国企、股份制企业、外企资产负债率上行(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2    采矿业为主要支撑，制造业利润结构持续分化 
采矿业利润增速继续上行，制造业利润增速回落， 电热气生产和供应业跌幅加深。三
大工业行业来看， 采矿业利润累计同比增速由34.9%继续上行至35.1%，为利润主要支
撑项。从细分行业来看，煤炭开采和洗选业利润累计同比增速上行 1.2个百分点至
51.6%，上行速度放缓，随天气温度下降，电力需求边际回落。煤炭开采行业内部供
需格局持续改善，前期成本持续压降给利润带来支撑。国际油价上行推动下， 石油和
天然气开采业增速反弹上行。有色金属矿采选业增速 高位 持续回落， 高库存带来一定
压制；黑色金属矿采选业增速由正转负，非金属矿采选业增速跌幅加深， 回落幅度相
对最大， 地产、 建筑开工偏弱， 给上游黑色金属、 非金属矿采选业带来一定拖累。 制
造业利润总额累计同比由18.8%进一步回落至17.4%， 产成品周转天数与上月持平。电

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

制造业新旧动能转换特征持续，AI依然为主要驱动，消费品制造业利润表现分化。从
制造业细分行业来看，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业跌幅加深， 非金属矿物制品跌幅边
际收敛， 地产投资偏弱的影响持续。 有色金属冶炼及压延加工业利润同比回落，整体
仍处高位。8月原油价格环比再度走高，美伊局势仍有较大不确定性，油价预期维持
高位震荡， 从下游相关产业链来看， 化学纤维制造业高位持平，纺织服装、 服饰业跌
幅持续收窄；化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续回落，地产链条相关品种需求偏弱，
农产品产业链等品种前期扩产过多， 景气度持续回落。 中游设备制造业整体利润回升，
电气机械及器材制造业利润跌幅收窄，通用设备制造业增速回升， 仅专用设备制造业
增速由正转负，外需仍具韧性，出口需求支撑持续。下游制造业利润增速表现分化，
计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增速由105%进一步上行至109.9%，AI高速发展，
价格与需求上行共同推动行业利润走高； 铁路、 船舶、 航空航天和其他运输设备制造
业增速由12.2%回落至10.9%， 汽车制造业由-20.4%回升至-16%。消费品制造业表现分
化，酒、饮料和精制茶制造业利润跌幅相对最大，高基数带来一定影响，食品制造、
农副食品加工业增速进一步放缓；家具制造业增速反弹上行， 但增速仍处低位；医药、
烟草、文娱用品制造业利润增速边际修复，内需偏弱依然带来一定拖累。

3    企业库存持续累积 
“量价齐升”驱动营收上行，企业库存持续累积。营业收入在工业生产回暖、 工业品
价格推动下回升。工业产成品存货累计同比由10.8%继续上行至11.0%， 库存持续累积，
但累库速度放缓。内需依然偏弱， 上游资源品涨价给下游企业带来成本压力， 企业现
金流和利润空间仍面临一定约束。 未来仍需关注PPI以及上下游行业价格剪刀差变化，
中下游制造业企业成本刚性问题短期难以迎来实质性缓解。 新经济利润表现预期持
续好于旧经济。海外政策不确定性指数在8月边际回落，但在地缘政治风险持续，油
价高位的影响下， 未来仍存在一定波动， 海外货币政策短期收紧的趋势不变。 我国政
策仍将注重稳经济，四季度财政资金有望逐步落地，“六张网”将发挥重要的支撑作
用。

## Document 181: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-09).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

## Document 193: A股三季度复盘：市场风格已悄然切换

Source PDF: all report pdf/A股三季度复盘：市场风格已悄然切换.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛格切换的本质是资⾦ “ 集体缩短久期 ” 。受⽆⻛险利率抬升影响，市场不再为远期的 “ 想象
空间 ” 买单，转⽽追逐当期的 “ 确定性 ” 。上半年⼤涨的科技股重挫，资⾦⼤举涌⼊煤炭、银
⾏等年内能算清账的红利资产。接下来， 10 ⽉三季报将成为后市的核⼼定价锚。
摘要：
9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，三季度最后⼀个交易⽇。
沪指收 3842.19 点，涨 0.31% ；深证成指跌 0.11% ，创业板指跌 0.23% ，科创 50 跌 2.51% 。两市
成交 1.45 万亿元。
单看这⼀天，什么都没发⽣。放进整个季度，轮廓才清楚：创业板指三季度跌 27.8% ，科创 50
跌 30.7% ，双双创下各⾃历史上最⼤的单季跌幅。同期，煤炭涨 19.29% ，⽯油⽯化涨 15.32% ，
农林牧渔涨 15.10% ，银⾏涨 14.56% 。 31 个申万⼀级⾏业， 12 个上涨， 19 个下跌。
⼀句话来说，三季度的市场⻛格，本质是⼀次 “ 集体缩短久期 ” 。
图：同⼀季度，两种久期，两种命运。注： “ 久期 ” 的概念详⻅下⽂。

也就是说，同⼀份未来现⾦流，被按⼀个更⾼的利率重新算了⼀遍，仓位随之从 " 远 " 搬到
了 " 近 " 。
01  上半年的赢家，⼏乎就是三季度的输家
最刺眼的对照在电⼦板块，上半年涨 86% ，三季度跌 32.65% ，落差超过 118 个百分点。通信上
半年涨 74% ，三季度跌 28.72% 。建筑材料上半年涨 46% ，三季度跌 26.10% ，原因落在同⼀件事
的两⾯：它是半导体上游材料涨价的受益者，涨价预期停下来，估值就跟着停下来。
跌幅榜前列：电⼦ -32.65% 、通信 -28.72% 、建筑材料 -26.10% 、电⼒设备 -23.73% 、机械设
备 -21.11% 、基础化⼯ -13.56% 、国防军⼯ -12.59% 、有⾊⾦属 -10.46% 、计算机 -8.13% 。
涨幅榜前列：煤炭 19.29% 、⽯油⽯化 15.32% 、农林牧渔 15.10% 、银⾏ 14.56% 、医药⽣物
10.12% 、房地产 9.36% 、交通运输 6.44% 、美容护理 6.07% 、⻝品饮料 4.34% 。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

图：上半年的赢家，⼏乎就是三季度的输家
图： 12 涨 19 跌，两端隔着⼀整个景⽓周期
两份榜单⼏乎是彼此的镜像。
有机构统计，申万 31 个⼀级⾏业上半年与三季度的涨跌幅相关系数为 -0.74 。轮动不会这么整
⻬，这更像是反转。

### Page 3

这三个⽉，基本⾯并没有掉头。中报⼝径下，中证⼈⼯智能指数成分股的归⺟净利润（ TTM ）
合计 1128.58 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 70.27% ；中证全指半导体指数成分股合计 942.92 亿元，同⽐增⻓
119.75% 。
业绩还在，甚⾄称得上强劲。可⻅，变化动因不在于业绩，⽽是市场愿意为这些业绩付多少
钱。
把时间拉⻓到前三季度，反差更⼤。
科创 50 累计上涨 13.82% ，是唯⼀收红的宽基指数；同期创业板指跌 2.12% ，深证成指跌
4.71% ，上证指数跌 3.19% ，北证 50 跌 27.83% 。同⼀个科技指数，年内还涨着 13.82% ，单个季
度却跌掉 30.70% 。创业板指的年内涨幅在 9 ⽉ 28 ⽇正式转负，⽽在 6 ⽉ 25 ⽇，它⼀度达到
36.49% 。三个⽉，从 +36.49% 到负数。
02  贴现率⼀动，先跌的是现⾦流最靠后的那批公司
三季度的问题，债券市场早就给过定义。
债券⾥有个指标叫久期，衡量价格对利率的敏感度：期限越⻓、现⾦流越靠后，久期就越⼤，

利率稍微⼀动，价格抖得越厉害。股票没有这个官⽅指标，但道理相通。⼀家公司的价值，是
它未来所有现⾦流折算回今天的总和；折现⽤的那个利率⼀旦抬升，被削得最狠的，是最后那
⼏年的权重。
2026 年 9 ⽉ 16 ⽇（当地时间），美联储把联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间上调 25 个基点，⾄ 3.75%—
4.00% 。这是 2023 年以来、也是两年降息之后的第⼀次转向。点阵图⾥， 18 名委员中有 16 ⼈预
计年底前⾄少还要加⼀次息，年底利率中值从 3.8% 上修⾄ 4.1% 。
图：折现率⼀抬，被削得最狠的是最后那⼏年
反应最先出现在债市。 9 ⽉， 10 年期美债收益率上⾏约 55 个基点⾄ 5.29% ，创 2007 年以来最⾼；
30 年期升⾄ 5.62% ，是 2002 年 6 ⽉以来的新⾼。
作为全球资产定价的锚，⽆⻛险利率站到 5% 以上，所有资产的 " 及格线 " 都被抬⾼了。

### Page 4

⼀家 2029 年才可能兑现利润的公司，和⼀家今年就能分红的公司，在 3% 的利率环境⾥，估值可
以很接近；但当⽆⻛险利率变成 5% ，前者必须拿出⾼得多的增⻓，才能证明⾃⼰值得被持有。
三季度，市场就是在做这道重新计算。
03  拥挤度到了极限，杠杆也到了极限
外部利率解释了 " 为什么是这个季度 " ，内部结构解释了 " 为什么跌得这么急 " 。
⼆季度，科创 50 单季涨 75.74% ，创业板指涨 36.35% 。 6 ⽉， TMT 板块成交额占全市场的⽐重触
及 43% 的历史极值。有机构测得，截⾄ 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，创业板指相对中证红利指数的阶段性超额收益
达到 48.6% ；到 9 ⽉初，这部分超额基本回吐完毕。
杠杆资⾦同步到位。
6 ⽉ 23 ⽇， A 股两融余额⾸次突破 3 万亿元；上半年融资融券新开⼾ 96.06 万⼾，同⽐增加
56.96% 。
跌的过程并不平缓。 9 ⽉ 24 ⽇，沪指失守 3900 点，超 4300 只个股下跌；中秋休市后， 9 ⽉ 28 ⽇沪

指单⽇跌 1.66% ，创业板指跌 4.53% ，全市场超 4500 只个股下跌，深证成指与创业板指双双跌破
7 ⽉前低，主要指数多数失守半年线。
然后是三季度的退潮。
两融余额从⾼点回落，成交额把这个过程记录得最完整： A 股⽇均成交额从 6 ⽉的 3.13 万亿元，
降到 7 ⽉的 2.70 万亿元、 8 ⽉的 2.25 万亿元、 9 ⽉的 1.83 万亿元； 9 ⽉ 29 ⽇缩⾄ 1.42 万亿元，为近
14 个⽉新低，较今年 1 ⽉ 14 ⽇ 3.99 万亿元的阶段天量萎缩逾六成。
图：退潮有刻度：⽇均成交额逐⽉下台阶
想买的⼈， 6 ⽉⾥已经买完了。剩下的只有卖。

### Page 5

下跌也不均匀。 9 ⽉，创业板指跌 8.82% 、科创 50 跌 9.17% ，微盘股指数上涨 1.65% 。杀估值集
中在权重成⻓股⾝上。
6 ⽉底，全市场还有 199 只年内收益翻倍的主动权益基⾦；到三季度末，只剩下 2 只。同⼀时期，
偏股混合基⾦前三季度的平均收益率为 2.59% ，仍为正，但基⾦之间的⾸尾业绩差距被拉开到
163.56 个百分点。
同⼀场回调⾥，有⼈守住了，有⼈没有。
04  钱搬去了 " 今年就能算清 " 的地⽅
从⾼处撤出来的钱，没有离场，它换了个位置站着。
政策先给了⼀条线。
" 反内卷 " 从 2025 年底中央经济⼯作会议开始部署， 2026 年 3 ⽉写进政府⼯作报告，明确要⽤产能
调控、标准引领、价格执法、质量监管等⼿段整治内卷式竞争； 5 ⽉ 13 ⽇，市场监管总局启动为
期 8 个⽉的专项⾏动。
落到⾏业上：光伏发布 5 项强制性国家标准，其中 3 项能耗能效标准将于 2027 年 1 ⽉ 1 ⽇实施； 8
家多晶硅⻰头签署⾃律倡议，承诺销售价格不低于核算成本；汽⻋⾏业把 60 天账期从⾏业倡议

升级为国家层⾯的规范；⽔泥在东北、西南、华东等区域连续发布涨价函，单次上调 20 元⾄ 50
元 / 吨。
价格确实开始动了。 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，秦皇岛港 Q5500 动⼒煤平仓价 983 元 / 吨，鄂尔多斯坑⼝含税价
760 ⾄ 830 元 / 吨； 8 ⽉我国进⼝动⼒煤 2763 万吨，同⽐下降 11% 。
政策的另⼀半落在需求端。 9 ⽉ 29 ⽇，财政部、央⾏、⾦融监管总局联合发布通知，⾃ 2026 年
10 ⽉ 1 ⽇起实施居⺠购房贷款贴息，政策实施期暂定 1 年，可享受贴息的贷款规模最⾼ 100 万元，
财政按年化 1 个百分点贴息，贴息期限最⻓ 5 年。房地产板块三季度上涨 9.36% ，涨的正是这条
贴息政策的确定性。
资⾦这条线，主⻆是险资。
险资已连续第五个季度加仓 A 股。到 2026 年 6 ⽉末， 42 家 A 股上市银⾏平均市净率 0.56 倍，全部
处于破净状态，按 2025 年分红测算有 13 家股息率超过 5% 。华泰证券的测算显⽰，保险⾏业上半
年已配置约 2.1 万亿元红利股，仍⽋配 1.9 万亿元。

政策给的是价格下限，险资给的是买盘下限。这两样东西有⼀个共同点：都能在今年的账上被
验证。
05  但 " 近处 " 也分真伪：农业涨 15% ，猪价跌到 10 元
涨幅榜第三的农林牧渔，最需要拆开看。
三季度涨 15.10% ，⽽它对应的现货价格，整个季度⾛得并不好。 9 ⽉⽣猪价格震荡下⾏， " ⾦
九 " 旺季落空， 9 ⽉底跌⾄约 10 元 / 公⽄的年内新低。供给端集中出栏、⼤猪压栏释放，需求端节
⽇备货偏弱，供需错配贯穿全⽉。
真正被买⼊的，是产能去化的预期。

### Page 6

⼆季度末，全国能繁⺟猪存栏调减⾄ 3780 万头，回到产能调控的绿⾊合理区间； 9 ⽉底前降⾄
3750 万头，被明确为硬指标，头部企业被要求带头压产、控体重。这属于政策承诺，还没有变
成当期利润。
它的逻辑能否成⽴，要等四季度猪价（能否）真的抬起来。
银⾏涨的逻辑⼜是另⼀套。
新会计准则下，⾼股息股票可以被指定为其他综合收益（ OCI ）类资产，市值波动不进利润表，
只有股息计⼊；低利率环境下，险资需要这样的现⾦流。这是资产负债表层⾯的再平衡。
图： “ 近处 ” 也分真伪：预期、事实与账⽬
⼀⾔以蔽之，在猪价上是预期，在煤价上是事实，在银⾏股息上是账⽬。
三者被⼀起买⼊，但只有后两者能在今年的报表上被验证。
06 10 ⽉，闸⻔先落在三季报上
9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的盘⾯，托住沪指的是酿酒、⾦融和医药；同⽇，科创 50 跌 2.51% ，盘中刷新本轮调整
新低。资⾦在同⼀个交易⽇⾥，把票投给了两种不同的时间。
接下来，估值的闸⻔将落到三季报上。
10 ⽉，上市公司将陆续披露三季报，这是四季度最重要的定价锚。

中报的强劲能否延续，此前 70.27% 的利润增速能否守住，直接决定科技⾏情是重建还是继续消
化。申万宏源策略的判断是，年内最好的⼀波进攻性机会，可能出现在 10 ⽉三季报消化估值之
后。
外部变量同样绕不开。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

前，海外利率的扰动⾄少摆在明处。
三季度的下跌，把⼀个问题摆得很清楚：当⽆⻛险利率站上 5% ，市场不再为 " 想象空间 " 付钱，
只为 " 能收到的时间 " 付钱。⾄于这个偏好能持续多久， 11 ⽉和 12 ⽉的报表会说话。

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Y tế và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -4,944 tỷ đồng, 
tập trung ở các mã VPB, TCB và VHM; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 1995 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -5,280 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 3,630 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 208 hợp đồng nhưng đang 
duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,428 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ hai liên tiếp sau nâng hạng, khi VN-Index lùi 
về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710.

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,

## Document 204: CGコードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ ～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能～

Source PDF: all report pdf/CGコードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ ～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

My Opinion

2026 年 9 月 29 日

1/6 
CG コードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ 
～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能 ～ 
シニア・フェロー（非常勤）  水口 啓子 
（℡：050-5469-1527）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年7 月に公表・施行された改訂コーポレートガバナンス・コード（CG コード）（以下、
コード）は、ガバナンス改革の実質化を重視し、形式的な対応にとどまらない、本質的なガ
バナンスの構築・運営を促している。プライム市場・スタンダード市場の上場会社について
は、コードの対象となる各原則について、実施するか、実施しない場合にはその理由を説明
するコンプライ・オア・エクスプレインが求められており、改訂コードを反映したコーポレ
ートガバナンス報告書の提出期限は2027 年 7 月末日となっている。 
○ コードでは、単なる制度や体制の記載にとどまらず、それらが実効性のある取締役会運営や
企業価値向上にどのようにつながっているのか、自社の経営環境や課題に即して説明するこ
とが求められる。コードは、単にコンプライとするだけでなく、具体的な取組みの内容や、
原則を実施していると評価する合理的な理由を説明することも、建設的な対話に資するとし
ている。 
○ コードは、取締役会の役割・責務として、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経
営資源の配分について、具体的な取組みを説明し、その配分が経営戦略に照らして適切かを
継続的に検証することを重視している。その実践に当たっては、どのリスクを取って成長を
目指し、どのリスクを抑制するかを取締役会が見極めることが重要となる。具体的には、設
備投資、研究開発、人的資本、無形資産等への成長投資と経営資源の配分について検証する
ことを求めるとともに、リスク管理体制に関する考慮事項として、サイバーセキュリティ、
地政学的要因によるサプライチェーン途絶、技術等の情報流出への対応を明記している。 
○ また、 こうした取締役会の機能強化に向け、コードの解釈指針では、独立社外取締役の役割 ・
責務、質・数・独立性の確保を重視するとともに、審議を活性化する観点から、取締役会事
務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機能強化が追記された。 
○ 上場企業を取り巻く事業環境の非連続な変化に伴う新たな事業機会・リスクへの迅速な対応
の重要性が高まる中で、今回の改訂は、企業と投資家双方の取組みを通じてガバナンス改革
の実質化を促している。本稿では、コードの全体像を俯瞰した上で、取締役会の実効的な監
督が経営判断にどのように寄与し得るかを踏まえて、投資家との質の高い対話へどのように
つなげ得るかを考察する。

### Page 2

2/6 
1. はじめに 
2026 年 7 月 21 日に、コーポレートガバナンス・コード（CG コード）（以下、コード）の改訂版
が公表・施行された。2015 年の導入以降、2018 年、2021 年と改訂を重ねてきたコードは、今回の
改訂によって一つの転換点を迎えたと考えられる。改訂を経てコードは、 ガバナンス体制のあり方
や具体的な対応を細かく規定するのではなく、 各社が自らの経営課題や事業環境に即して、 原則の
趣旨・精神に沿ったガバナンスを実践することを重視する方向へ軸足を移す内容となっている。事
業環境が非連続に変化し、その変化のサイクルも短期化する中、 迅速な経営判断の必要性が高まっ
ている。コーポレートガバナンスを「形式的な体制整備」から「経営判断の質を高め、中長期的な
企業価値向上につなげる仕組み」へと捉え直すものといえる。コードの全体像と主な論点は、以下
の通りである（資料１）。

資料 1 2026 年改訂コードの構成と主な論点・着眼点 
基本原則 主なテーマ 主な論点・着眼点 
基本原則 1 
株主の権利・平等性 の
確保、株主との対話 
株主が判断に必要な情報を得て権利を行使できる環境を整える。特に、
有価証券報告書の株主総会前提出を重要な例として示すとともに、少数
株主の利益保護、政策保有株式の検証、株主との建設的な対話を重
視。 
基本原則 2 
株主以外のステークホ
ルダーとの適切な協働 
持続的な成長を支える従業員や取引先等との協働を重視。解釈指針で
は、人的資本への投資と適切な分配、公正・適正な取引を例示。多様性
の確保や企業年金のアセットオーナーとしての機能発揮も対象。 
基本原則 3 
適切な 情 報 開 示 と 透
明性の確保 
財務情報に加え、戦略・リスク・ガバナンス等の非財務情報を、ひな型的
な記述にとどめず、正確で分かりやすく有用な形で示すことを重視。開示
を株主との建設的な対話の基盤と位置付ける。 
基本原則 4 取締役会等の責務 
成長の道筋を示し、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経営
資源配分を説明・継続的に検証することを重視。独立社外取締役による
監督、取締役会事務局の機能強化、サイバー・地政学リスク等への対応
を通じて取締役会の実効性を高める。 
（出所）東京証券取引所「コーポレートガバナンス・コード～会社の持続的な成長と中長期的な企業価値の向上のために～」
（2026 年 7 月 21 日）を参照の上、第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

今回の改訂では、 取締役会が成長の道筋を示し、 成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む
経営資源の配分について、 具体的な取組みを説明するとともに、 その妥当性を継続的に検証するこ
とが重視されている。具体的には、①取締役会の在るべき姿について不断の検討を行うこと、②独
立社外取締役の質と実効性を高めること、③取締役会事務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機
能を強化することなどが重要なテーマとなっている。なお、 今回の改訂では対話環境の整備として

### Page 3

3/6 
有価証券報告書の総会前開示なども明記されている。 
企業価値を左右する経営上の重要課題について、 取締役会がどのような問いを投げかけ、 経営陣
との議論を通じて意思決定の質の向上につなげるかが重要である。 今後は、 コーポレートガバナン
ス報告書においても、実質的なガバナンスに関する記載への注目が高まることが想定される。 
本稿では、取締役会の実効性を「取締役会における議論が、経営上の重要な意思決定の質や、企
業の持続的価値創造にどのように寄与し得るか」 という観点から捉える。2026 年に改訂されたコー
ドの全体像を踏まえた上で、取締役会における「監督」の在り方を整理し、その具体的な実践領域
として、資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ選択のあり方を取り上げる。さらに、独立社外取締役や取
締役会事務局の役割に触れつつ、 取締役会の実効性をいかに高め、 投資家との質の高い対話へつな
げるかを考察する。

2．取締役会による経営の方向付けと実効的な監督 
コードの改訂が示唆するように、取締役会の本質的な役割は、会社の進むべき方向を示し、経営
陣がリスクと機会を多角的に捉えて適切にリスクテイクできる環境を整えるとともに、 独立した客
観的な立場から経営を監督することにある（資料2）。

資料 2 取締役会の役割・責務に関わる諸視点 
項目 主な視点・検討事項 
企業戦略の方向付け 
・経営理念に基づき、成長戦略・経営計画を策定する。 
・資本コストを意識し、収益性・資本効率の目標を明確化する 。 
・成長投資、M&A、人的資本・知的財産への投資、事業ポートフォリオの見直しな
ど、経営資源の配分を具体的に示す。 
・中期経営計画未達の原因と対応策を検討し、株主に説明する。 
適切なリスクテイクの支援 
・経営陣の健全な起業家精神に基づく 提案を歓迎しつつ、客観的・多角的に検討
する。 
・経営資源が成長戦略に沿って有効活用されているかを継続的に検証する。 
・中長期的な企業価値向上につながる報酬制度・インセンティブを設計する。 
経営陣への実効的な監督 
・経営陣の業績を客観的に評価し、必要に応じて選解任に反映する。 
・特に CEO の後継者計画（サクセッションプラン）を取締役会が主体的に監督す
る。 
・適時・正確な情報開示を監督する。 
・支配株主や経営陣との利益相反・関連当事者取引を適切に管理する。 
内部統制・リスク管理 
・グループ全体の内部統制・リスク管理体制を整備し、運用状況を監督する。 
・サイバーセキュリティ、地政学的なサプライチェーン途絶、技術 等の情報流出な
どのリスクにも対応する。

4/6 
サステナビリティを巡る取組
み 
・中長期的な企業価値の向上の観点から、サステナビリティを巡る取組みについ
て基本的な方針を策定・適切な対応を行う。 
（出所）東京証券取引所「コーポレートガバナンス・コード～会社の持続的な成長と中長期的な企業価値の向上のために～」
（2026 年 7 月 21 日）を参照の上、第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

実効的な取締役会を考察するに際しては、重要な戦略課題に議論を集中させ、経営陣に「有用な
問い」 を投げかけることが大いに期待される。 「何を取締役会が自ら判断し、 何を経営陣に委ねるの
か」という委任の境界を明確にし、経営陣の意思決定の前提、リスク選好、リスク抑制方針・施策
を多角的に検証することで、意思決定の質を高めることが期待される。 
中期経営計画の進捗、 主要な前提条件の変化などを継続的にモニタリングし、 計画が未達となっ
た場合には、 その要因を独立的な視点から分析して、 次の戦略や資本配賦に反映させることが肝要
である。持続的な企業価値創造に向けて、取締役会がリスクテイクを支援・監督する際も、ストレ
スシナリオを含め、 成長の源泉や市場規模の見立て、 競争優位性の持続可能性を踏まえた検討が必
要となる。加えて、上場会社のグローバル化がさらに進展する中、グループ全体のリスク管理・内
部統制の重要性は高まっている。 法的リスクや地政学リスク、 グローバル展開に伴う内部統制の実
効性に至るまで、多角的な問いを通じて、意思決定における前提、リスクなどを検証する姿勢が重
要となる。

3．資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ改革の合理性の検証 
取締役会の監督機能が試される代表的な領域が、 資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ改革であり、 投
資家にとっても大きな関心事項である。コードは、資本コストを意識し、収益性・資本効率の目標
を明確化することに加え、 成長投資、M&A、 人的資本・知的財産への投資、 事業ポートフォリオの見
直しなどについて言及している。取締役会は、どの時間軸で価値を生み出すのか、どの事業にどの
程度の経営資源を配分し、 リスク調整後にどの程度のリターンを見込むのかを、 全社的かつ多角的
な視点から判断することが求められる。 
事業ポートフォリオを検証する際には、ROIC（投下資本利益率）と WACC（加重平均資本コスト）
の比較が有用な判断材料となる。ただし、これらは取締役会が活用し得る指標の一例であり、その
比較のみで事業の継続・撤退を決めることは適切ではない。投資期間やリスク、将来の選択肢とし
ての価値に加え、将来の成長率、追加投資に対する限界リターン、構造改革の余地、事業間のシナ
ジー、撤退コストなども踏まえて、企業価値の向上に向けて評価することが重要である。様々な参
考指標が想定される中で、投下資本の規模を考慮した経済的利益（Economic Profit 、以下「EP」）
は、ROIC だけでは捉えにくい価値創造の絶対額を見る補助的な参考指標となり得る。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 価値創造（EP：Economic Profit）の考え方

（出所）経済産業省「成長投資ガイダンス ―価値創造の拡大を通じた企業の持続的成長に向けて―」（2026 年）

EP において注目されるのは、ROIC を高めることにとどまらず、資本コストを超えて生み出され
る「価値の絶対額」に着目する点にある。ROIC が高い事業であっても、投下資本が小さければ、 当
該事業が生み出す EP の絶対額は小さくなり得る。また、成長余地のある事業において、追加投資
によってROIC がWACC を上回る状態を維持・拡大できる見込みがある場合には、 投下資本の拡大に
伴って EP の絶対額を拡大できる可能性がある。 一方で、 足元でROIC が高い事業だけに資本を集中
させることが、 必ずしも企業価値の極大化につながるとは限らない。 将来の成長余地や追加投資に
よる限界リターンの動向などを総合的に見極めることも肝要であろう。加えて、現時点で ROIC が
WACC を下回る事業についても、 直ちに撤退を決めるのではなく、 構造改革によって収益性を改善で
きる可能性を検討する余地がある。その際には、追加投資の規模や改革に要する期間・費用、他の
事業に経営資源を振り向けた場合の機会費用も踏まえて判断する必要がある。財務的な視点や定量
的なデータも踏まえ、事業戦略の妥当性を多角的に検証する取締役会での議論に期待したい。

4．独立社外取締役と取締役会事務局――実効性を支える基盤 
コードは、取締役会は社外取締役による問題提起を含め自由闊達で建設的な議論・意見交換を尊
ぶ気風の醸成に努めるべき旨を指摘している。取締役会が実効的な議論を展開するためには、 それ
を支える体制と環境の整備が不可欠である。独立社外取締役は、経営陣との間で緊張感のある信頼
関係を構築した上で、 経営陣に執行を委ねつつ、 監督や助言を通して取締役会における大局的な議
論や意思決定を行い、経営陣の説明が是認できない場合には、 反対意見を述べることも役割上必要
となる。監督と執行の間の情報の非対称性、監督と執行の境目の曖昧さ、さらには経営スピードと
監督の深さとのトレードオフなど、構造的な課題もある中で、独立社外取締役は、執行側から独立
した立場を保ちつつ、 業務執行の過度な細部に介入することなく、 適切な距離感を保って監督を行
うことが期待される。 
こうした課題に対して、 コードが言及する 「取締役会事務局 （コーポレートセクレタリー等） 」 の

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
機能強化が果たし得る役割は大きい。 強化された取締役会事務局による年間アジェンダの設計、 議
題の重要性に応じた審議時間の配分、取締役への情報提供、事前説明における論点整理、取締役会
での議論結果のフォローアップまでを一連のプロセスとして捉えることで、 取締役会の時間を企業
価値に直結する議論へ振り向ける余地が生まれる。 取締役会が個別案件の単なる承認作業から解放
され、企業戦略の方向付け、適切なリスクテイクの支援、内部統制・リスク管理などの重要テーマ
を踏まえた経営陣への実効的な監督に時間 を集中させることが可能となっているかが重要な論点
となる。 
例えば、独立社外取締役は、 「投資判断の前提となる市場成長率や競争優位性、期待ROIC は妥当
か」 「現在保有する事業を持ち続ける合理性はあるか」 「投資判断の前提が崩れた場合、いつ経営資
源の配分を見直すか」 といった問いを投げかけることができる。 こうした議論が経営資源の配分や
事業ポートフォリオの判断にどう影響したかは、 取締役会の実効性を考える上で重要な視点となる。
投資家と経営陣や社外取締役との対話においても、 「取締役会で何が議論され、どのように経営判
断に影響したか」という観点からの対話が視野に入る。

5．小括 
2026 年改訂 CG コードは、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経営資源の配分につい
て、 取締役会が戦略との整合性を継続的に検証することを重視している。 こうした役割を実効的に
果たすには、 独立社外取締役を含む取締役会の構成員が、 経営陣とは異なる視点から問いを投げか
け、議論を深めることが重要である。その結果、取締役会の意思決定プロセスそのものが重要とな
り、経営資源の配賦の規律について説明を求められる場面も増えていくことが想定される。 コード
の改訂を受けたコーポレートガバナンス報告書においても、監督と執行それぞれが果たす役割を踏
まえながら、どのように実効性のある取締役会運営がなされ得るのかについて、 説明の質が問われ
ることも考えられる。 
本コードと「スチュワードシップ・コード」は、車の両輪であり、両者が適切に相まって実効的
なコーポレートガバナンスが実現されることが期待される。コーポレートガバナンスの改善を強く
意識している長期保有の投資家は、コードの改訂を受けた開示を切り口に、取締役会が企業固有の
経営課題について問いを設定し、 その問いが持続的な企業価値向上にどう結び付いているかを確認
する機会を得ることも想定される。 
コード改訂を受けたコーポレートガバナンス報告書では、 成長戦略に沿って経営資源をどのよう
に配分し、 その妥当性を取締役会がいかに検証しているかについて、 各社の状況に即した説明が期
待される。原則を実施する場合にも、実施しない場合にも、その判断と具体的な取組みを丁寧に示
すことが、投資家との建設的な対話につながる。取締役会による監督の実効性が高まり、企業の成
長の道筋と資源配分の合理性を巡る対話が深まることを期待したい。

FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

Chart of the Week / 7 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds amid 
Australia-US interest rate differentials and positive 
outlook for the AUD

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure over

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

The memory segment is projected to account for 
two-thirds of global semiconductor revenues by

## Document 317: Polish rate hike odds are rising, but policymakers are in no rush

Source PDF: all report pdf/Polish rate hike odds are rising, but policymakers are in no rush.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The tone of the NBP Governor’s press conference was broadly
in line with our forecast, assuming the next hike in ﬁrst quarter
2027. He recognised rising inﬂation risks linked to geopolitical
tensions and solid domestic demand. There are concerns of
higher energy prices feeding into the economy. The central
bank appears willing to wait to raise rates

Rafal Benecki and Adam Antoniak
In today's press conference, National Bank of Poland Governor Glapiński
acknowledged higher inﬂation but seems willing to wait to raise interest rates
During his October press conference, National Bank of Poland (NBP)
Governor Adam Glapiński highlighted growing risks to the inﬂation
outlook. At the same time, however, he stressed that the rise in
prices so far has been driven by an external supply shock and that
there is little evidence of higher energy costs spreading broadly to
the prices of other goods and services.
Global sources of inﬂation

### Page 2

Explaining the rationale behind the October decision to leave
interest rates unchanged, NBP Governor Adam Glapiński stressed
that the global economy is facing an energy shock resulting from
the conﬂict in the Middle East. The conﬂict is becoming more
protracted, and its inﬂationary eﬀects are intensifying. Polish
inﬂation rose above the upper bound of the NBP's tolerance band
around its target in September (2.5%; +/-1 percentage point), but it
should return to that range in October.

Local inﬂationary factors are in play
In the governor's view, the inﬂation outlook is subject to greater
uncertainty due to the geopolitical tensions. Moreover, the cuts to
fuel excise duty and VAT are temporary and, at present, are
expected to remain in place until the end of 2026.
Adam Glapiński also listed a number of risk factors that could
contribute to broader-based price increases:
1. Consumer demand is solid, while wage growth in the corporate

sector is running at around 6% year-on-year.
2. Economic conditions are improving in Poland's external
environment, including in Germany.
3. Global agricultural commodity prices are rising, which could lift
inﬂation in Poland in the future.
4. Fiscal policy remains expansionary.
5. Strong growth in producers’ prices (PPI) may signal mounting
cost pressures.
He therefore reiterated that the main Monetary Policy Council (MPC)
objective is to prevent higher fuel prices from spilling over into other

prices across the economy.
Inﬂation still primarily driven by a supply
shock, no second-round effects
Balancing the risks outlined above, the MPC chairman also
emphasised that almost all the increase in inﬂation to date has
resulted from higher fuel prices. In September, energy accounted for
60% of inﬂation, while the pass-through from energy prices to
Cookie settings

### Page 3

other prices has remained limited. The increase in core inﬂation
excluding food and energy since the beginning of the year has
been modest, and core inﬂation probably edged down slightly in
September. There are also currently no signs of second-round
eﬀects.
Monetary policy guidance
Future central bank decisions will depend on incoming data and the
shape of the November macroeconomic projection. Governor
Glapiński does not currently see a need for an interest rate increase

at the next meeting in November, although he did not rule one out
entirely. He added that the MPC stands ready to act should inﬂation
risks continue to mount.
Our rates view still assumes 50bp hikes in
1Q27
The tone of the governor's press conference suggests that the MPC
remains patient and is not inclined towards a rapid and decisive
tightening of monetary policy for as long as inﬂation continues to
be driven by an external shock and there are no visible signs of

higher energy prices feeding more broadly into the prices of other
goods and services.
In our view, the Council still has time to respond, and the tone of the
October conference was in line with our monetary scenario. If the
energy shock proves more persistent, interest rate increases could
come in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, when we expect two 25bp hikes.
We see a risk of a modest increase in core inﬂation around the turn
of 2026-27, which in our view would warrant a precautionary rise in

interest rates.

## Document 322: Quarterly Market Outlook

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quarterly Market Outlook.pdf
Report source: Sony FG
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026年9月30日発行
2026年9月号
Quarterly
Market
Outlook

### Page 2

2026/9/30Quarterly Market Report
2
日本の金融政策見通し：（井上）
米国の金融政策見通し（井上）
＜メインシナリオ（需要主導のインフレ圧力へ
シフト）：緩やかな利上げ（確率60％）＞
 FRBは、9月の見通しで、経済が力強さを増したと
評価した一方、インフレ率の2％目標への収斂に
時間を要する点を懸念
 FOMCメンバーは2026年中にも う1回の利上げを
見込む一方、2027年中は1回の利上げと現状維
持ないし利下げに2分。この間、市場は合計3回を
超える利上げを織り込み
 2026年中は、既往のエネルギー価格上昇の国内
への波及リスクに対して12月に1回の利上げを行
い、2027年には需要主導のインフレ圧力を抑制す
る目的でもう1回の利上げを行い、政策金利（FF
レートの誘導目標）は4.25～4.5％へ
 その後は、政策金利が明確に引き締め領域に入
るだけに、FRBも経済や物価の反応を見極めるた
め、政策金利を現状維持
＜代替シナリオ②（インフレ圧力が早期に沈

静化）：利上げを停止（同15％）＞
 エネルギー価格の迅速な低下によって、供給要因
による国内物価への波及が迅速に収束
 需要主導のインフレ圧力は残存するが、2026年12
月の利上げによって4％台に達する政策金利によ
り十分引き締め的と判断して現状維持に転換
 資産価格のオーバーシュートとその後の急速な調
整によって、経済の下方リスクがむしろ増大
 基調的インフレのアンカーがポイント
＜メインシナリオ（基調的インフレ率が目標に
収斂）：政策金利の速やかな中立化（確率
60％）＞
 日銀は、9月利上げの際に、経済の底堅さに自信
を示す一方、基調的インフレの上振れリスクを意識
 植田総裁は、基調的インフレを2％近傍に収斂させ
ることを主眼とする「新たな局面」に入ったとの認識
 本年12月にさらに25bpの利上げを行い、年末には
1.50％。2027年3月にも追加利上げで政策金利は
1.75％へ到達
 ただし、政策金利が明確に中立化する中で、経済
活動への負荷が徐々に明確になる可能性

 日銀によるビハインドザカーブの懸念が後退する
中で、長期金利の焦点は財政運営（消費減税の財
源等）や米国の長期金利による影響へ
＜代替シナリオ①（インフレ圧力が想定以上
に継続）：利上げを継続（同25％）＞
 景気が底堅い下で、エネルギー価格の高止まりに
よる国内物価への波及が強まりつつ継続
 インフレ期待を抑制するため、FRBは2027年中に
利上げを継続し、2027年末に5％近傍へ上昇
 経済や物価への影響だけでなく、金融環境のタイ
ト化が非直線的に出現するリスク
＜代替シナリオ②（経済の回復が想定以上に
遅延）：利上げの減速（同10％）＞
 輸出の好調が内需に波及せず、経済活動が停滞
 円安の抑制も必要であるため、日銀はより緩やかな
ペースの利上げにシフト
 日米金利差の拡大による円安圧力が輸入インフレ
→実質購買力低下→一層の内需停滞を招くリスク
＜代替シナリオ①（エネルギー価格の高騰が
想定以上に継続）：金融引き締めの領域へ（同
30％）＞
 エネルギー価格の高止まり、ないし円安の継続に

よって国内物価への波及が想定以上に長く継続
 基調的インフレの上振れを阻止するため、日銀は利
上げを継続し、ターミナルレートは2％台前半に上昇
 経済と物価のトレードオフが深刻化し、政府の経済
政策との調整が困難化

米国物価見通し（渡辺）
＜ポイント＞
 関税や中東情勢に伴うエネルギー高によりコア財
物価が上振れ（上図）
 長期インフレ予想は安定しており、賃金・サービス
物価への二次的波及は限定的。エネルギー高が
ホームメード・インフレに発展するリスクは限定的
 労働生産性の上昇が単位労働コストを抑え 、基
調的な物価を抑制する見込み（下図）

 供給要因のインフレは27年前半に剥落へ。ただし、
堅調な米景気がディマンドプル ・イ ンフレを 招き、
27年以降も物価は2％目標に着地しない公算
＜リスク＞
 AI関連の需要増で電子部品や電力の価格を中心
にインフレ上振れ
 AIによる生産性向上のディスインフレ圧力
 イラン紛争早期終結で油価下落、インフレ下振れ
 トランプ大統領がFRBの独立性を脅かし、金融政
策の信認と物価抑制効果が低下する恐れ
 政府債務増大とドル価値の低下に伴う インフレ
＜ポイント＞
 現状の物価は政府の支援策により上値が抑えら
れている格好（上図）。同対応策の終了を想定す
れば、中東情勢悪化によるインフレ圧力により 、
コアCPIは26年度末に3％近傍に加速する公算
 今後の中東情勢の小康状態を想定し 、食料品の
消費減税による下押し効果もあり 、27年度のコア
CPIは減速に向かう と 予想。28年度以降は減税
効果剥落により 再び加速へ。減税効果を除けば 、
物価は2％目標圏内で推移すると予想

 長期インフレ予想は足下 2％程度（下図）。今後
は中東情勢悪化による影響剥落や物価目標達
成への信認から目標圏で推移と予想
＜リスク＞
 原油高や財政悪化懸念、それに伴う 円安が再度
進めば物価上振れリスク大
 食料品の消費減税に関わる政策の詳細が不透
明で、物価への影響も 不確実性が大きい
注：コアCPIは生鮮食品除くベース
インフレ率は2027年以降も2％目標に着地しない公算
出所：BEA、FRB、SFGI

市場予想
（左軸）
（期待政策金利、％）
日本の金利見通し（宮嶋）
米国の金利見通し（渡辺）
＜ポイント＞
 日銀はインフレ上振れ防止のため利上げをペー
スアップ 。先行き は、12月・来年3月に利上げ予
想（金融政策の頁参照） 。その後の政策金利は
基調物価の2％前後の推移の下で横ばいを想定
 長期金利は 、利上げペースアップ等から 上昇に
歯止め。ただし 、米金利高や日銀国債保有減少
等から、3％近傍の高水準継続と予想（上図）
 超長期金利は世界的な財政懸念等が逆風も、発

行減額等への期待から小幅に低下へ （下図）
＜リスク＞
 想定以上のイ ンフレ加速と なれば、政策金利が
2％を超え、イールドカーブ上方シフトのリスク
 グローバルな財政懸念や高市政権の日銀利上
げ牽制姿勢が強まれば 、長・超長期ゾーンの金
利上昇が加速（スティープ化）
＜ポイント＞
 長期金利＝期待政策金利（政策金利予想）＋ター
ムプレミアム（国債保有リスク）（上図）
 タームプレミアムの上昇要因は、トランプ政策を巡
る 不確実性から、財政不安や国債の需給悪化懸
念、中間選挙後の不透明感へシフト。AI企業の大
量起債に伴う投資家資本の争奪戦も一因
 期待政策金利が上昇。イ ラ ン 紛 争 に 伴 うコ ス ト プ ッ
シュ・インフレと 堅調な経済指標がFRBを タカ 派化さ
せ、市場の利上げ期待を押し上げる（下図）
＜リスク＞
 イラン紛争早期終結で原油安、金利低下
 インフレや財政悪化、トランプ氏のFRBに対する政
治圧力等による「悪い金利上昇」
 ウォ ーシュFRB議長のフォワードガイダンス曖昧化

で、金融市場のボラティリティが上昇
 FRBの資産圧縮の加速でタームプレミアム急上昇
 金利上昇による景気後退の結果、金利下振れ
米国：長期金利＝期待政策金利＋タームプレミアム
注：期待政策金利とタームプレミアムはNY連銀のACMモデルの推計値

ドル円レート

（左軸）
ドル円の見通し（尾河）
＜ポイント＞
 前四半期から全体的にドル円の長期見通しを
小幅ながら円高方向に修正
 今年の円安トレンドのテーマであった日銀の
「利上げの遅れ（Behind the Curve）」は後退
 日米の利上げは、共にあと2回程度と 予想。日
米の金利差が著し く開く、或いは縮小はせず、

今後1 年程度は155 円を 中心とし た上下10円
程度のボックス相場が続くと 予想
 一方で、今後デジタル赤字がさらに膨らむ可
能性など 、構造的な円安要因は残るため （下
図）、その後は緩やかな円安トレンドが継続
＜リスク ＞
 日本の財政懸念が再燃することにより長期金
利の上昇を伴った円安が加速するリスク
 トランプ政策による米財政懸念により 、国債価
格、株価、ドルのトリプル安が進むリスク
 米中間選挙後も 、トランプ大統領の 「ドンロー
主義」による諸外国との摩擦が続き 、米国の
信認低下を伴うドルの大幅安が進行するなど

ユーロの見通し（森本）

ユーロドル 1.10 1.12 1.13 1.17 1.20
ユーロ円 163 179 185 191 198
＜ポイント＞
 中東情勢を受けたエネルギー価格の上昇と
物価見通し の上方修正を背景に、ECBは3年
ぶり の利上げを実施。ただユーロ圏景気は低
成長が続いており、追加利上げ余地は限定的
 ユーロドルは長期的には独米実質金利差と の
連動性を維持 。当社は26/12と 27/3の米利上
げを 予想し ており、金利差の観点から来年前
半までは上値が重い展開を予想（上図）
 その後は米利上げ局面の一巡に加え 、ドイ ツ
の国防・インフラ支出拡大 （下図）による景気
押し 上げから 金利差は縮小へ。ユーロ圏の経
常黒字も 下支えとなり、27年後半以降はユー

ロの緩やかな上昇を見込む 。独国防省予算
は27年以降大幅な増額が計画されている
＜リスク ＞
 エネルギー高の長期化やフランスの財政不安
再燃により、景気悪化や域内の信用不安が強
まれば、ユーロが大きく下振れる恐れ
【独財政支出対名目GDP比】

連邦軍特別基金の合計。2027年は政府予算案、2028～30年は財政計画。名目GDPは2025
年実績を起点に、連邦政府の名目GDP成長率見通しから算出

## Document 373: Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ First Estimate (2026-10-09)

Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok
